A^{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}A^{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}=-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{2}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}-\sum_{\alpha=0}^{2}A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}.

(5.8)

To prove (
5.7
), we first establish the
0
-order estimate. A direct calculation on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
shows that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\nabla d\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x^{\prime}}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\nabla d\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

By using Lemma
5.2
, (
5.4
) and (
5.5
), we can prove that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}.

(5.9)

Next, we will prove the
(s_{0}-\frac{1}{4})
-order estimate. Operating the derivative of
\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}
(
1\leq|\beta|\leq s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}
) on (
5.8
) and integrating it on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we get

\begin{split}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma}}&\lesssim\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}+|I_{1}|+|I_{2}|,\end{split}

(5.10)

where

\begin{split}&I_{1}=-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\cdot\Lambda^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau,\\
&I_{2}=-\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau.\end{split}

Write
I_{1}
as

\begin{split}I_{1}=&-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{\mathbf{U}})-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau\\
&+\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{t}\phi\cdot\partial_{3}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau,\\
=&-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}[\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}]\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau\end{split}

Similarly,
I_{2}
can be written by

\begin{split}I_{2}=&-\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}})-A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau\\
&+\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big(A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\big)\cdot\partial_{\alpha}(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau.\end{split}

By using Lemma
2.4
, we infer that

\begin{split}|I_{1}|&\lesssim\big(\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\|d\partial_{t}\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\sup_{\theta,r}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\partial_{t}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\end{split}

(5.11)

and

\begin{split}|I_{2}|\lesssim&\big(\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\|d\nabla\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\sup_{\theta,r}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}d\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\\
&+\big(\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(5.12)

Take sum of
1\leq\beta\leq s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}
on (
5.10
). By using Lemma
5.2
, (
5.11
), (
5.12
), (
5.5
), and (
5.4
), we obtain

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(5.13)

Operating
\nabla
on (
5.6
), we have

\begin{split}A^{0}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{t}(\nabla\mathbf{U})+\sum^{2}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{i}(\nabla\mathbf{U})=-\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\nabla(A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U}))\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U},\end{split}

In a similar way, we can obtain

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\nabla\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(5.14)

Thanks to
\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}=-\sum^{2}_{i=1}(A^{0})^{-1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}
and Lemma
5.3
, we can carry out

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(5.15)

The estimate (
5.15
) together with (
5.9
), (
5.13
), (
5.14
) thus gives (
5.7
). This conclude the proof of this lemma.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
. Let
\mathbf{U}
satisfy the assumption in Lemma
5.4
. Then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\lambda(\mathbf{U}-P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U}),dP_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U},\lambda^{-1}d\nabla P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(5.16)

Proof.

Let
\Delta_{0}
be a standard multiplier of order
0
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, such that
\Delta_{0}
is additionally bounded on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Clearly, we have

A^{0}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{t}(\Delta_{0}U)+\sum^{2}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{i}(\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U})=-[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U}.

Applying Lemma
5.4
, we derive

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{0}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}.

(5.17)

Due to commutator estimates, we obtain

\|[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}.

According to the inequality (
5.17
), it turns out

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}.

(5.18)

To get a bound for
\lambda(\mathbf{U}-P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U})
, we write

\lambda(\mathbf{U}-P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U})=\lambda\sum_{\mu\geq\lambda}P_{\mu}\mathbf{U},

where
P_{\mu}\mathbf{U}
satisfies the above conditions for
\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}
. Applying (
5.18
) and replacing
s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}
to
s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}
, we get

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\lambda(\mathbf{U}-P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}=&\sum_{\mu\geq\lambda}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mu P_{\mu}\mathbf{U}\cdot\frac{\lambda}{\mu}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

Taking square of the above inequality, which yields

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\lambda(\mathbf{U}-P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Finally, applying (
5.17
) to
\Delta_{0}=P_{<\lambda}
and
\Delta_{0}=\lambda^{-1}\nabla P_{<\lambda}
shows that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|dP_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\lambda^{-1}d\nabla P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Therefore, the estimate (
5.16
) holds. We have finished the proof of this lemma.
∎

Taking advantage of Lemma
5.4
, inequalities (
4.4
) and (
4.5
), we can directly obtain:

Corollary 5.6
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
. Suppose
(\mathbf{v},\rho,w)\in\mathcal{H}
. Then the following estimate holds:

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\rho|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Next, let us prove the characteristic energy estimates for vorticity.

Lemma 5.7
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
. Suppose that
(\mathbf{v},\rho,w)\in\mathcal{H}
. Then we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|w|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\nabla w|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(5.19)

Proof.

By a change of coordinates
x_{2}\rightarrow x_{2}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
and setting
\tilde{w}(t,x)=w(t,x^{\prime},x_{2}+\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
, the third equation in (
1.22
) is transformed to

\partial_{t}\tilde{w}+\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla\tilde{w}=-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{2}\tilde{w}-\tilde{v}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{j}\tilde{w}.

For
\phi
is independent of
x_{2}
, we then get

\partial_{t}\tilde{w}+\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\nabla\tilde{w}=-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{2}\tilde{w}-\tilde{v}^{1}\partial_{1}\phi\partial_{1}\tilde{w}.

(5.20)

multiplying with
\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\alpha}\tilde{w}
, and integrating on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\tilde{w}\|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}\leq&C\|\nabla\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\tilde{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}+C\|d\phi-dt\|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\|\tilde{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

Similarly, using (
2.3
), we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\nabla{w}\|^{2}_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\leq&C\|\nabla\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\tilde{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+C\|d\phi-dt\|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\|\tilde{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\\
&+C\|d\phi-dt\|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\|\tilde{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}{\|\nabla\tilde{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{8}_{x}}}(1+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}).\end{split}

Taking advantage of inequalities (
4.3
) and (
4.5
), we find

\|\nabla{w}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

(5.21)

and

\|{w}\|_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(5.22)

Due to
\partial_{x^{\prime}}w=\nabla w\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi
, we obtain

\|\partial_{x^{\prime}}{w}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|\nabla{w}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}.

(5.23)

Combining (
5.21
), (
5.22
), with (
5.23
) yields the estimate (
5.19
). We complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

Finally, we are ready to prove Proposition
5.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

From Lemma
5.5
, it only remains for us to verify that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

Due to Lemma
5.4
, (
5.4
), and (
5.5
), we get

\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\rho|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

By using the definition of
\mathbf{g}
(see (
4.1
)), and Lemma
5.3
, the following estimate

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|c_{s}^{2}-c_{s}^{2}(0)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon_{2},\end{split}

holds for
s_{0}>\frac{7}{4}
. Therefore, the estimate (
5.1
) holds. This concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

It also remains for us to obtain Proposition
4.2
. To achieve the goal, we need to introduce a new frame on the null hypersurface
\Sigma
.

5.2.
Null frame

We introduce a null frame along
\Sigma
as follows. Let

V=(dr)^{*},

where
r
is the defining function of the foliation
\Sigma
, and where
*
denotes the identification of covectors and vectors induced by
\mathbf{g}
. Then
V
is the null geodesic flow field tangent to
\Sigma
. Let

\sigma=dt(V),\qquad l=\sigma^{-1}V.

Thus
l
is the
\mathbf{g}
-normal field to
\Sigma
normalized so that
dt(l)=1
, hence

l=\left<dt,dx_{2}-d\phi\right>^{-1}_{\mathbf{g}}\left(dx_{2}-d\phi\right)^{*}.

Therefore, the coefficients
l^{j}
are smooth functions of
\mathbf{v},\rho
and
d\phi
. Conversely, we have

dx_{2}-d\phi=\left<l,\partial_{2}\right>^{-1}_{\mathbf{g}}l^{*}.

(5.24)

Seeing from (
5.24
),
d\phi
is also a smooth function of
\mathbf{v},\rho
and the coefficients of
l
.

Next we introduce the vector fields
e_{1}
tangent to the fixed-time slice
\Sigma^{t}
of
\Sigma
. We do this by applying Grahm-Schmidt orthogonalization in the metric
\mathbf{g}
to the
\Sigma^{t}
-tangent vector fields
\partial_{1}+\partial_{1}\phi\partial_{2}
.

Finally, we denote

\underline{l}=l+2\partial_{t}.

It follows that
\{l,\underline{l},e_{1}\}
form a null frame in the sense that

\displaystyle\left<l,\underline{l}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=2,\qquad\qquad\ \ \left<e_{1},e_{1}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=1,

\displaystyle\left<l,l\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=\left<\underline{l},\underline{l}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=0,\quad\left<l,e_{1}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=\left<\underline{l},e_{1}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=0.

The coefficients of each fields is a smooth function of
\mathbf{v},\rho
and
d\phi
, and by assumption we also have the pointwise bound

|e_{1}-\partial_{1}|+|l-(\partial_{t}+\partial_{2})|+|\underline{l}-(-\partial_{t}+\partial_{2})|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

Based on the above setting, we introduce a result about the decomposition of curvature tensor.

Lemma 5.8
.

(
[
49
]
, Lemma 5.8)
Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
. Suppose
f
satisfying

\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}f=F.

Let
(t,x^{\prime},\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
denote the projective parametrisation of
\Sigma
, and for
0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 1
, let
/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}
denote differentiation along
\Sigma
in the induced coordinates. Then, for
0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 1
, one can write

/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\beta}(f|_{\Sigma})=l(f_{2})+f_{1},

where

\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\|df\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}}+\|df\|_{L^{4}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\|F\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}.

Corollary 5.9
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
and
\delta_{0}\in(0,s_{0}-\frac{7}{4})
. Let
R
be the Riemann curvature tensor for the metric
{\mathbf{g}}
. Let
e_{0}=l
. Then for any
0\leq a,b,c,d\leq 2
, we can write

\left<R(e_{a},e_{b})e_{c},e_{d}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}|_{\Sigma}=l(f_{2})+f_{1},

(5.25)

where
|f_{1}|\lesssim|\nabla w|+|d\mathbf{g}|^{2}
and
|f_{2}|\lesssim|d{\mathbf{g}}|
. Moreover, the characteristic energy estimates

\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

(5.26)

holds. Additionally, for any
t\in[-2,2]
, it follows

\|f_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(5.27)

Proof.

Due to the definition of curvature tensor, we have

\left<R(e_{a},e_{b})e_{c},e_{d}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=R_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}e^{\alpha}_{a}e^{\beta}_{b}e_{c}^{\mu}e_{d}^{\nu},

where

R_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\partial^{2}_{\alpha\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}+\partial^{2}_{\beta\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\mu}-\partial^{2}_{\beta\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\nu}-\partial^{2}_{\alpha\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\mu}\right]+F(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},d\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\beta}),

where
F
is a sum of products of coefficients of
\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}
with quadratic forms in
d\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\beta}
. Due to Lemma
5.8
, the estimate (
5.25
) holds. Next, we will prove (
5.26
) and (
5.27
).

Applying Proposition
5.1
, the term
F
satisfies the bound required of
f_{1}
. It suffices for us to consider

\frac{1}{2}e^{\alpha}_{a}e^{\beta}_{b}e_{c}^{\mu}e_{d}^{\nu}\left[\partial^{2}_{\alpha\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}+\partial^{2}_{\beta\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\mu}-\partial^{2}_{\beta\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\alpha\nu}-\partial^{2}_{\alpha\nu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\mu}\right].

We therefore look at the term
e^{\alpha}_{a}e_{c}^{\mu}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}
, which is typical. By (
5.3
) and Proposition
5.1
, we get

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|l^{\alpha}-\delta^{\alpha 0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\underline{l}^{\alpha}+\delta^{\alpha 0}-2\delta^{\alpha 2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|e^{\alpha}_{1}-\delta^{\alpha 1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(5.28)

By using (
5.28
) and Proposition
5.1
, the term
e_{a}(e_{c}^{\mu})\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}
satisfies the bound required of
f_{1}
, we therefore consider
e_{a}(e_{c}(\mathbf{g}_{\beta\nu}))
. Since the coefficients of
e_{c}
in the basis
/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}
have tangential derivatives bounded in
L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)
, we are reduced by Lemma
5.8
to verifying that

\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Note
\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}=\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}
. By use of Corollary
5.6
and Lemma
5.7
, we have

\begin{split}\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{g}_{\mu\nu}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\ \|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\rho\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|(d\mathbf{v},dg)\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dg)\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+[2-(-2)]^{\frac{1}{4}}\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

Above,
\nabla w
and
(d{\mathbf{g}})^{2}
are included in
f_{1}
. Therefore, we have finished the proof of this corollary.
∎

Based on the above null frame, we can discuss the estimates for connection coefficients as follows.

5.3.
Estimates for connection coefficients

Define

\chi=\left<D_{e_{1}}l,e_{1}\right>_{\mathbf{g}},\qquad l(\ln\sigma)=\frac{1}{2}\left<D_{l}\underline{l},l\right>_{\mathbf{g}}.\quad

For
\sigma
, we set the initial data
\sigma=1
at the time
-2
. Thanks to Proposition
5.1
, we have

\|\chi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|l(\ln\sigma)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(5.29)

In a similar way, if we expand
l=l^{\alpha}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}
in the tangent frame
\partial_{t},\partial_{x^{\prime}}
on
\Sigma
, then

l^{0}=1,\quad\|l^{1}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(5.30)

Next, we will establish some estimates for connections along the hypersurface.

Lemma 5.10
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
and
\delta_{0}\in(0,s_{0}-\frac{7}{4})
. Let
\chi
be defined as before. Then

\|\chi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(5.31)

Furthermore, for any
t\in[-2,2]
,

\|\chi\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

(5.32)

Proof.

Along the null hypersurface,
\chi
satisfies a transport equation (see Klainerman and Rodnianski
[
30
]
). This tells us:

l(\chi)=\left<R(l,e_{1})l,e_{1}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}-\chi^{2}-l(\ln\sigma)\chi.

Due to Corollary
5.9
, we can rewrite the above equation by

l(\chi-f_{2})=f_{1}-\chi^{2}-l(\ln\sigma)\chi,

(5.33)

where

\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2},

(5.34)

and for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\|f_{2}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

(5.35)

Let
\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}
be defined in (
5.2
). To be simple, we set

F=f_{1}-\chi^{2}-l(\ln\sigma)\chi.

Applying integration by parts on (
5.33
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}(\chi-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\ &\|[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}},l](\chi-f_{2})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}F\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}.\end{split}

(5.36)

By using
s_{0}>\frac{7}{4}
,
H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})
is an algebra. This ensures that

\begin{split}\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}F\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}&\lesssim\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\chi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\\
&\quad+\|\chi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\cdot\|l(\ln\sigma)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\\
&\quad+\|\mu\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\cdot\|\chi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}.\end{split}

(5.37)

For
a\geq 0
, a direct calculation tells us

\begin{split}[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{a},l]f=&\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{a}(l^{\alpha}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}f)-l^{\alpha}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{a}f\\
=&\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{a}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}(l^{\alpha}f)-\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{a}(/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}l^{\alpha}f)-l^{\alpha}\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{a}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}f\\
=&-\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{a}(/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}l^{\alpha}f)+[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{a}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha},l^{\alpha}]f.\end{split}

As a result, we can bound
\|[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}},l](\chi-f_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}
by

\begin{split}\|[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}},l](\chi-f_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\leq&\|/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha}l^{\alpha}(\chi-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\\
&+\|[\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}/\kern-5.50003pt\partial_{\alpha},l^{\alpha}](\chi-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}.\end{split}

(5.38)

Note
l^{0}=1
and
s_{0}>\frac{7}{4}
. Applying Kato-Ponce’s commutator estimate and Sobolev embeddings, the right hand side of (
5.38
) can be bounded by

\|l^{1}(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\|\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}(\chi-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}.

(5.39)

Because of (
5.29
), (
5.30
), (
5.34
), (
5.36
), (
5.37
) and (
5.39
), we thus prove that

\sup_{t}\|(\chi-f_{2})(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(5.40)

The estimate (
5.40
) combining with (
5.34
) yield (
5.31
). Due to (
5.33
), we find

\begin{split}\|\chi-f_{2}\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}}&\lesssim\|f_{1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\chi^{2}\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|l(\ln\sigma)\chi\|_{L^{1}_{t}C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(5.41)

Using Sobolev’s imbedding, we have

H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow C^{\delta_{0}}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad\delta_{0}\in(0,s_{0}-\frac{7}{4}).

(5.42)

Taking advantage of (
5.35
), (
5.41
), and (
5.42
), we get (
5.32
). At this stage, we have finished the proof of this lemma.
∎

Finally, we present a proof for Proposition
4.2
as follows.

5.4.
The proof of Proposition
4.2

First, we will verify (
4.9
). By using (
5.1
) and (
5.24
) for
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{m}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}
, then the inequality (
4.9
) follows from the following bound:

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{2})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Above, it is understood that one takes the norm of the coefficients of
l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{2})
in the standard frame on
\mathbb{R}^{1+2}
. The geodesic equation, together with the bound for Christoffel symbols
\|\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|d{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}
, imply that

\|l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Therefore, it suffices for us to bound the tangential derivatives of the coefficients for
l-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{2})
in the norm
L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)
. By using Proposition
5.1
, we can estimate the Christoffel symbols

\|\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Note that
H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})
is a algebra when
s_{0}>\frac{7}{4}
. We thus derive

\|\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}e_{1}^{\beta}l^{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

In what follows, we can establish the following bound:

\|\left<D_{e_{1}}l,e_{1}\right>\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\left<D_{e_{1}}l,\underline{l}\right>\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\left<D_{l}l,\underline{l}\right>\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(5.43)

In fact, the first term in (
5.43
) is
\chi
, which can be bounded by using Lemma
5.10
. For the second term in the left side of (
5.43
), consider

\left<D_{e_{1}}l,\underline{l}\right>=\left<D_{e_{1}}l,2\partial_{t}\right>=-2\left<D_{e_{1}}\partial_{t},l\right>.

Then it can be bounded by use of Proposition
5.1
. Similarly, using Proposition
5.1
, we can obtain the desired estimate for the last term in (
5.43
). Therefore, the rest of proof is to obtain

\|d\phi(t,x^{\prime})-dt\|_{C^{1,\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

In order to do this, it suffices for us to show that

\|l(t,\cdot)-(\partial_{t}-\partial_{x_{2}})\|_{C^{1,\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

It’s clear that the coefficients of
e_{1}
are small in
C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})
perturbations of their constant coefficient analogs. Thus, it’s left for us to prove

\|\left<D_{e_{1}}l,e_{1}\right>(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}+\|\left<D_{e_{1}}l,\underline{l}\right>(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Above, the first term is bounded by Lemma
5.10
, and the second by using

\|\left<D_{e_{1}}\partial_{t},l\right>(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma^{t})}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Therefore, (
4.10
) holds. We complete the proof of Proposition
4.2
.

6.
The proof of Proposition
4.3
and Strichartz estimates

In this part, our goal is to give a proof of Proposition
4.3
and establish Strichartz estimates of solutions.

6.1.
The proof of Proposition
4.3

We first introduce Strichartz estimates for linear inhomogeneous wave equations.

Proposition 6.1
.

Assume
s\in(\frac{7}{4},2]
. Suppose that
(\mathbf{v},\rho,w)\in\mathcal{{H}}
and
G(\mathbf{v},\rho)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. For each
1\leq r\leq s+1
, then the linear, homogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}F=0,\\
&F(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=F_{0},\quad\partial_{t}F(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=F_{1},\end{cases}

is well-posed for the initial data
(F_{0},F_{1})
in
H_{x}^{r}\times H_{x}^{r-1}
. Moreover, the solution satisfies, respectively, the energy estimates

\|F\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}F\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}\leq C\big(\|F_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|F_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),

and the Strichartz estimates

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}F\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|F_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|F_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),\quad a<r-\frac{3}{4}.

(6.1)

The similar estimate (
6.1
) also holds if we replace
\left<\nabla\right>^{a}
by
\left<\nabla\right>^{a-1}d
.

Remark 6.1
.

The proof of Proposition
6.1
will be given in Section
7
.

Now we are ready to prove Proposition
4.3
by use of Proposition
6.1
.

Proof of Proposition
4.3
by using Proposition
6.1
.

Let
\mathbf{g}
be stated as in Proposition
4.3
. Let
V
satisfy the linear, homogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}V=0,\\
&V(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{0},\quad\mathbf{T}V(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{1}-\Theta(t_{0},x),\end{cases}

(6.2)

where
(f_{0},f_{1}-G(t_{0},x))\in H_{x}^{r}\times H_{x}^{r-1}
.
Let
Q
satisfy the the linear, nonhomogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}Q=\mathbf{T}\Theta+B,\\
&Q(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=0,\quad\mathbf{T}Q(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=\Theta(t_{0},x),\end{cases}

(6.3)

where
\Theta(t_{0},x)\in H_{x}^{r-1}
. Due to
\mathbf{T}=\partial_{t}+\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla
, we can rewrite (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) as

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}V=0,\\
&V(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}V(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{1}-\Theta(t_{0},x)-\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla f_{0}(t_{0},x),\end{cases}

and

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}Q=\mathbf{T}\Theta+B,\\
&Q(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=0,\quad\partial_{t}Q(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=\Theta(t_{0},x).\end{cases}

Therefore, the sum
f=V+Q
satisfies

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=\mathbf{T}\Theta+B,\\
&f(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{0}\in H_{x}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\\
&\mathbf{T}f(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{1}\in H_{x}^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).\end{cases}

Observe that the proof for (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) relies on
V
and
Q
. By Proposition
6.1
, we can infer

\|V\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}V\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}-\Theta(t_{0},\cdot)-(\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla)V(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),

and

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}V\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}-\Theta(t_{0},\cdot)-(\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla)V(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),\quad a<r-\frac{3}{4}.

Thanks to
s>\frac{7}{4}
and
r<s+1
, it turns out

\begin{split}&\|V\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}V\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}\\
\leq&C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}+\|\Theta(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\|\nabla f_{0}(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}\big)\\
\leq&C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}+\|\Theta\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),\end{split}

(6.4)

and

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}V\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}+\|\Theta\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),\quad a<r-\frac{3}{4}.

(6.5)

By applying Lemma
2.7
and Proposition
6.1
, we can also prove

\|Q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}Q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}\leq C\big(\|\Theta(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}+\|\Theta\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{r-1}}\big),

(6.6)

and

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}Q\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C\big(\|\Theta(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}+\|\Theta\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{r}}+\|B\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}\big),\quad a<r-1.

(6.7)

Adding (
6.4
) and (
6.6
), we get (
4.11
). Adding (
6.5
) and (
6.7
), we obtain (
4.12
). Therefore, we complete the proof of Proposition
4.3
.
∎

We can also obtain the following Strichartz estimates of solutions.

6.2.
Strichartz estimates for solutions

Proposition 6.2
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
. Suppose
(\mathbf{v},\rho,w)\in\mathcal{H}
and
G(\mathbf{v},\rho)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Let
\mathbf{v}_{+}
be defined in (
1.20
). Then for
a<s-\frac{3}{4}
, we have

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}\rho,\left<\nabla\right>^{a}\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|w_{0}\|_{H^{1+}}.

(6.8)

The similar estimate (
6.8
) holds if we replace
\left<\nabla\right>^{a}
by
\left<\nabla\right>^{a-1}d
.

Proof.

We note the functions
\rho
and
\mathbf{v}_{+}
satisfy the system

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}\rho=\mathcal{D},\\
&\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{v}_{+}=\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}+\mathbf{Q}.\end{cases}

(6.9)

By using the Strichartz estimate in Proposition
6.1
(taking
r=s
and
a<r-\frac{3}{4}
) , we obtain

\begin{split}&\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}\rho,\left<\nabla\right>^{a}\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{1}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}\\
\lesssim&\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+(\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{1+}}+\|d\rho,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{[-2,2]}L_{x}^{\infty}})\|\rho,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}.\end{split}

(6.10)

Operating
\Lambda_{x}^{a^{\prime}}
on (
2.3
), we have

\begin{split}\mathbf{T}(\Lambda_{x}^{a^{\prime}}\nabla w)=&[\Lambda_{x}^{a^{\prime}},\mathbf{T}]\nabla w+\Lambda_{x}^{a^{\prime}}(\nabla\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla w)\\
=&[\Lambda_{x}^{a^{\prime}},\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla]\nabla w+\Lambda_{x}^{a^{\prime}}(\nabla\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla w).\end{split}

By using commutator and product estimates, for
0\leq a^{\prime}<1.
, we can obtain the following energy:

\begin{split}\|w\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{1+a^{\prime}}}\lesssim&\|w_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{1+a^{\prime}}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\nabla\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|w\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{1+a^{\prime}}}d\tau+\int^{t}_{0}\|\nabla\mathbf{v}\|_{C^{a^{\prime}}_{x}}\|w\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{1}}d\tau.\end{split}

Due to Gronwall’s inequality, we get

\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{1+a^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|w_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{1+a^{\prime}}}\exp\left(\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{C^{a^{\prime}}_{x}}d\tau\right),\quad 0\leq a^{\prime}<1.

(6.11)

Due to (
4.4
), (
4.5
) and (
6.11
), the estimate (
6.10
) becomes

\begin{split}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}\rho,\left<\nabla\right>^{a}\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}&\lesssim\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|w_{0}\|_{H^{1+}},\qquad a<s-\frac{3}{4}.\end{split}

∎

The Proposition
6.2
states a Strichartz estimate of solutions with a very low regularity of the velocity, density, and vorticity. This also motivates the following proposition.

Proposition 6.3
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
and
\delta\in(0,s-\frac{7}{4})
. Suppose
(\mathbf{v},\rho,w)\in\mathcal{H}
and
G(\mathbf{v},\rho)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Let
\mathbf{v}_{+}
be defined in (
1.20
). Then we have

\begin{split}\|d\rho,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}&\lesssim\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+{\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{1+2\delta}}}.\end{split}

(6.12)

Furthermore, the Strichartz estimates

\|d\mathbf{v},d\rho,d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(6.13)

and the energy estimates

\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla w\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{8}_{x}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(6.14)

hold.

Proof.

Due to (
6.9
), and using the Strichartz estimate in Proposition
6.1
(taking
r=s
and
a=1
), we have

\begin{split}\|d\rho,d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+{\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{1+\delta}}}.\end{split}

(6.15)

Recall

\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{v}_{+}+\mathbf{v}_{-},\quad v^{i}_{-}=(-\Delta)^{-1}(\epsilon^{ia}\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\partial_{a}w).

By Sobolev imbedding
C^{\delta}_{x}\hookrightarrow H_{x}^{1+2\delta}
, we then get

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}&\lesssim\|\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\nabla w)\|_{H^{1+2\delta}}\\
&\lesssim\|\mathrm{e}^{\rho}\nabla w\|_{H^{2\delta}}\\
&\lesssim\|\rho\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|w\|_{H_{x}^{1+2\delta}}.\end{split}

(6.16)

Thanks to (
6.15
) and (
6.16
), we have proved (
6.12
). For
s_{0}>\frac{7}{4}
, using Theorem
2.10
, we conclude that

\begin{split}\|\nabla\rho,\nabla\mathbf{v}_{+},\nabla\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}&\lesssim\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+{\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}}\\
&\lesssim\|\rho_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|w_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla w_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}.\end{split}

(6.17)

By using (
4.3
) and (
6.17
), we have

\begin{split}\|\nabla\rho,\nabla\mathbf{v}_{+},\nabla\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(6.18)

Due to (
1.22
), it yields

\|\mathbf{T}\rho,\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\|\nabla\rho,\nabla\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}.

(6.19)

Note that
\epsilon_{3}\ll\epsilon_{2}
. Taking advantage of (
6.18
) and (
6.19
), we therefore obtain (
6.13
). By using Theorem
2.9
and (
6.13
), the estimate (
6.14
) holds. Therefore, we have finished the proof of this proposition.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

The Strichartz estimate (
6.12
) requires a very low regularity of the velocity, density, and vorticity. We hope that the regularity exponent in (
6.12
) is sharp.

It only remains for us to prove Proposition
6.1
. This will be discussed in the next section.

7.
Proof of Proposition
6.1

In this section, following Smith-Tataru’s paper
[
49
]
, we will present a proof for Proposition
6.1
by using wave-packet approximation, .

7.1.
The proof of Proposition
6.1

In order to prove Proposition
6.1
, we first reduce it to phase space. Given a frequency scale
\lambda\geq 1
(
\lambda=2^{j},j\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
), we consider the smooth coefficients

\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}=P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{g}=\sum_{\lambda^{\prime}<\lambda}P_{\lambda^{\prime}},

where
\mathbf{g}
is defined in (
4.1
). We now introduce a proposition in the phase space, which will be very useful.

Proposition 7.1
.

Suppose
(\mathbf{v},\rho,w)\in\mathcal{{H}}
and
G(\mathbf{v},\rho)\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Let
f
satisfy

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=0,\quad(t,x)\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
(f,\partial_{t}f)|_{t=t_{0}}=(f_{0},f_{1}).\end{cases}

(7.1)

Then for each
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{1}\times L^{2}
there exists a function
f_{\lambda}\in C^{\infty}([-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
, with

\mathrm{supp}\widehat{f_{\lambda}(t,\cdot)}\subseteq\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\frac{\lambda}{8}\leq|\xi|\leq 8\lambda\},

such that

\begin{cases}&\|\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{4}_{[-2,2]}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}),\\
&f_{\lambda}(-2)=P_{\lambda}f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}f_{\lambda}(-2)=P_{\lambda}f_{1}.\end{cases}

(7.2)

Additionally, if
r>\frac{3}{4}
, then the following Strichartz estimates holds:

\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{4}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\lambda^{r-1}(\|P_{\lambda}f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|P_{\lambda}f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

(7.3)

Next, we give a proof for Proposition
6.1
based on Proposition
7.1
.

Proof of Proposition
6.1
by use of Proposition
7.1
.

We divide the proof into several cases.

(i)
r=1
. Using basic energy estimates for (
1.24
), we have

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\ (\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}})\exp(\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\\
\lesssim&\ \|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Then the Cauchy problem (
7.1
) holds a unique solution
f\in C([-2,2],H_{x}^{1})
and
\partial_{t}f\in C([-2,2],L_{x}^{2})
. It remains to show that the solution
f
also satisfies the Strichartz estimate (
6.1
).

Without loss of generality, we take
t_{0}=0
. For any given initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{1}\times L^{2}
, and
t_{0}\in[-2,2]
, we take a Littlewood-Paley decomposition

f_{0}=\sum_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}f_{0},\qquad f_{1}=\sum_{\lambda}P_{\lambda}f_{1},

and for each
\lambda
we take the corresponding
f_{\lambda}
as in Proposition
7.1
. If we set

f=\sum_{\lambda}f_{\lambda},

then
f
matches the initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})
at the time
t=t_{0}
, and also satisfies the Strichartz estimates (
7.3
). In fact,
f
is also an approximate solution for
\square_{\mathbf{g}}
in the sense that

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

We can derive the above bound by using the decomposition

\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}+\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}.

The first term can be controlled by Proposition
7.1
. As for the second term, using
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, we can rewrite

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}=\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}({\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}})\nabla df_{\lambda}.\end{split}

By using Hölder’s inequality, it follows that

\begin{split}\|\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\left(\lambda\|{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)\left(\sum_{\lambda}\|df_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(7.4)

Due to Bernstein’s inequality, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\left(\lambda\|{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)\lesssim&\ \mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\big(\lambda\sum_{\mu>\lambda}\|\mathbf{g}_{\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\big)\\
\lesssim&\ \mathrm{sup}_{\lambda}\big(\lambda\sum_{\mu>\lambda}\mu^{-(1+\delta)}\|d\mathbf{g}_{\mu}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}\big)\\
\lesssim&\ \|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}(\textstyle{\sum_{\mu}}\mu^{-\delta})\lesssim\ \|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.5)

Combining (
7.4
) and (
7.5
), we get

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum_{\lambda}}\square_{\mathbf{g}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).\end{split}

For given
F\in L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
, we set

\mathbf{M}F=\int^{t}_{0}f^{\tau}(t,x)d\tau,

where
f^{\tau}(t,x)
is the approximate solution formed above with the Cauchy data

f^{\tau}(\tau,x)=0,\quad\partial_{t}f^{\tau}(\tau,x)=F(\tau,\cdot).

By calculating

\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{M}F=\int^{t}_{0}\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{\tau}(t,x)d\tau+F,

it follows that

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{M}F-F\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}f^{\tau}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.

Using the contraction principle, we can write the solution
f
in the form

f=\tilde{f}+\mathbf{M}F,

where
\tilde{f}
is the approximation solution formed above for initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})
specified at time
t=0
, and

\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

The Strichartz estimates now follow since they holds for each
f^{\tau}
,
\tau\in[0,t]
. By Duhamel’s principle, we can also obtain the Strichartz estimates for the linear, nonhomogeneous wave equation

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=F^{\prime},\\
f|_{t=0}=f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}f|_{t=0}=f_{1}.\end{cases}

That means

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}f\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|F^{\prime}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}},\quad a<\frac{1}{4}.

(ii)
1<r\leq s+1
. Based on the above result, we plan to transform the initial data in
H^{1}\times L^{2}
. Operating
\left<\nabla\right>^{r-1}
on (
7.1
), we have

\square_{\mathbf{g}}\left<\nabla\right>^{r-1}f=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\nabla\right>^{r-1}]f.

Let
\left<\nabla\right>^{r-1}f=\bar{f}
. Then
\bar{f}
is a solution to

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}\bar{f}=-[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\nabla\right>^{r-1}]\left<\nabla\right>^{1-r}\bar{f},\\
(\bar{f}(t_{0}),\partial_{t}\bar{f}(t_{0}))\in H^{1}\times L^{2}.\end{cases}

(7.6)

To handle this case, we need to bound the right term as

\|[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\nabla\right>^{r-1}]\left<\nabla\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\nabla\right>^{m}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}),

(7.7)

provided
m>1-s
. To prove it, we apply analytic interpolation to the family

\bar{f}\rightarrow[\square_{\mathbf{g}},\left<\nabla\right>^{r-1}]\left<\nabla\right>^{1-r}\bar{f}.

For
\mathrm{Re}z=0
, noting
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, we use the commutator estimate (c.f. (3.6.35) of
[
26
]
) to get

\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\nabla\right>^{z}]\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i},\left<\nabla\right>^{z}]\partial_{i}(\partial_{\alpha}\bar{f})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

For
\mathrm{Re}z=s
, we use the Kato-Ponce commutator estimate

\begin{split}\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\nabla\right>^{z}]\left<\nabla\right>^{-z}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}=&\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i},\left<\nabla\right>^{z}]\left<\nabla\right>^{-z}\partial_{i}(\partial_{\alpha}\bar{f})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{-z}\nabla d\bar{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(7.8)

Taking advantage of

\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0},

we can bound (
7.8
) by

\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta},\left<\nabla\right>^{z}]\left<\nabla\right>^{-z}\nabla^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\nabla\right>^{-z}\nabla d\bar{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}).

Let us go back (
7.6
). Using the discussion in case
r=1
, for
\theta<0
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\|\left<\nabla\right>^{(\theta-1)}d\bar{f}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|\left<\nabla\right>^{r-1}f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\left<\nabla\right>^{r-1}f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\nabla\right>^{-r}\nabla d\bar{f}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}),\\
\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}+\|d\bar{f}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\left<\nabla\right>^{-r}\nabla d\bar{f}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}).\end{split}

(7.9)

Taking
\theta=-r+1
in (
7.9
), we can see

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{-r}\nabla d\bar{f}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}).

(7.10)

By (
7.6
), (
7.7
), and (
7.10
), we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}f,\left<\nabla\right>^{a-1}df\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\ \epsilon_{0}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\quad a<r-1.\end{split}

∎

Remark 7.1
.

From Proposition
7.1
, it implies that there is a good approximate solution
f_{\lambda}
for the problem

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f=0,\quad(t,x)\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
(f,\partial_{t}f)|_{t=t_{0}}=(P_{\lambda}f_{0},P_{\lambda}f_{1}).\end{cases}

(7.11)

In the case of
\epsilon_{0}\lambda\leq 1
, we can take
f_{\lambda}=P_{\lambda}f
, where
f
is the exact solution of (
7.11
).
Applying energy estimates for (
7.11
), we can see

\|df\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|P_{\lambda}f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|P_{\lambda}f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}.

(7.12)

Moreover, for
\mathbf{g}^{00}_{\lambda}=-1
, so we have

\begin{split}\|\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\|[\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}_{\lambda},\partial_{\alpha}P_{\lambda}]\partial_{i}f\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{2}_{x}}+\|P_{\lambda}(\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha i}_{\lambda})\partial_{i}f\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}(\|P_{\lambda}f_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|P_{\lambda}f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).\end{split}

The Strichartz estimate (
7.3
) follows from Sobolev imbeddings and (
7.12
). Hence, the rest of the paper is to establishing Proposition
2.10
in the case that

\epsilon_{0}\lambda\gg 1.

7.2.
Proof of Proposition
7.1

By using Remark
7.1
, it suffices for us to consider the problem if the frequency
\lambda
is large enough, namely

\lambda\geq\epsilon_{0}^{-1}.

In this case, we can construct an approximate solution to (
7.1
) by using wave packets. We also note that the proposition
7.1
has two parts: (
7.2
) and (
7.3
). The conclusion of the first part, i.e. (
7.2
) will be established in the following Proposition
7.5
and
7.6
. The inequality (
7.3
) will be proved in Proposition
7.8
.

Before our proof, we introduce a spatially localized mollifier
T_{\lambda}
by

T_{\lambda}f=\chi_{\lambda}*f,\quad\chi_{\lambda}=\lambda^{2}\chi(\lambda^{-1}y),

where
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is supported in the ball
|x|\leq\frac{1}{32}
, and has integral
1
. By choosing
\chi
appropriately, any function
\phi
with frequency support contained in
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|\xi|\leq 4\lambda\}
can be factored
\phi=T_{\lambda}\widetilde{\phi}
, where
\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\approx\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}
.

7.2.1.
A normalized wave packet

Let’s begin with the definition of a normalized wave packet.

Definition 7.1

(
[
49
]
,Definition 8.1)
.

Let the hypersurface
\Sigma_{\omega,r}
and the geodesic
\gamma
be defined in Section
5
. A normalized wave packet centered around
\gamma
is a function
f
of the form

f=\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\lambda^{-\frac{5}{4}}T_{\lambda}(uh),

where

u(t,x)=\delta(x_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,r}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega})),\quad h=h_{0}((\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}(x^{\prime}_{\omega}-\gamma^{\prime}_{\omega}(t))).

Here,
h_{0}
is a smooth function supported in the set
|x^{\prime}|\leq 1
, with uniform bounds on its derivatives
|\partial^{\alpha}_{x^{\prime}}h_{0}(x^{\prime})|\leq c_{\alpha}
.

Remark 7.2
.

The advantage of this definition is that the derivatives of wave packets involve only the tangential behavior of the restrictions of various functions to the characteristic surfaces
\Sigma_{\omega,r}
, as opposed to their regularity within the support of the wave packet. Therefore, we don’t need to discuss the behavior of the null foliations
\cup_{r\in\mathbb{R}}\Sigma_{\omega,r}
in
transversal directions.

We give two notations here. We denote
L(\varphi,\psi)
to denote a translation invariant bilinear operator of the form

L(\varphi,\psi)(x)=\int K(y,z)\varphi(x+y)\psi(x+z)dydz,

where
K(y,z)
is a finite measure. If
X
is a Sobolev spaces, we then denote
X_{\kappa}
the same space but with the norm obtained by dimensionless rescaling by
\kappa
,

\|\varphi\|_{X_{\kappa}}=\|\varphi(\kappa\cdot)\|_{X}.

Since
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
, we get
s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}>\frac{1}{2}
. For
\kappa<1
, we have
\|\varphi\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{\kappa}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbb{R})}
.

Based on the above definition, let us calculate what we will get when we operate
\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}
on wave packets.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
f
be a normalized packet. Then there exists another normalized wave packet
\tilde{f}
and functions
\phi_{m}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega}),m=0,1,2
, so that

\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}P_{\lambda}f=L(d\mathbf{g},d\tilde{P}_{\lambda}\tilde{f})+\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\lambda^{-\frac{5}{4}}P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}\sum_{m=0,1,2}\psi_{m}\delta^{(m)}(x^{\prime}_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,r}),

(7.13)

where the functions
\psi_{m}=\psi_{m}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega})
satisfy the scaled Sobolev estimates

\|\psi_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{a,x^{\prime}_{\omega}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}\lambda^{1-m},\quad m=0,1,2,\quad a=(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(7.14)

Proof of Proposition
7.2
.

For brevity, we consider the case
\omega=(0,1)
. Then
x_{\omega}=x_{2}
, and
x^{\prime}_{\omega}=x^{\prime}
. We write

\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}P_{\lambda}f=\lambda^{-1}([\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]+P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}})(uh).

(7.15)

For the first term in (
7.15
), noting
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}
supported at frequency
\leq 4\lambda
, then we can write

[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]=[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]\tilde{P}_{\lambda}\tilde{T}_{\lambda}

for some multipliers
\tilde{P}_{\lambda}
and
\tilde{T}_{\lambda}
which have the same properties as
P_{\lambda}
and
T_{\lambda}
. Therefore, by using the kernal bounds for
P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}
, we conclude that

[\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}},P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}]f=L(d\mathbf{g},df).

For the second term in (
7.15
), we use the Leibniz rule

\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}(uh)=h\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}u+(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})\partial_{\alpha}u\partial_{\beta}h+u\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}h.

(7.16)

Let
\nu
denote the conormal vector field along
\Sigma
. We thus know
\nu=dx_{2}-d\phi(t,x^{\prime})
. In the following, we take the greek indices
0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq 1
.

For the first term in (
7.16
), we can compute out

\begin{split}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}u=&\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}\delta^{(2)}_{x_{2}-\phi}-2(\partial_{2}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}\delta^{(1)}_{x_{2}-\phi}\\
&+(\partial^{2}_{2}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}\delta^{(0)}_{x_{2}-\phi}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\phi\delta^{(1)}_{x_{2}-\phi}\\
&+\partial_{2}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\phi\delta^{(0)}_{x_{2}-\phi}.\end{split}

Above,
\delta^{(m)}_{x_{2}-\phi}=(\partial^{m}\delta)(x_{2}-\phi)
. Due to Leibniz rule, we can take

\begin{split}\psi_{0}&=h\big\{(\partial^{2}_{2}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}+(\partial_{2}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\phi\big\},\\
\psi_{1}&=h\big\{2(\partial_{2}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}-\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}\phi\big\},\\
\psi_{2}&=h(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta})\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta},\end{split}

Taking advantage of (
4.9
), Proposition
5.1
, and Corollary
5.6
, we can conclude that this settings of
\psi_{0},\psi_{1},
and
\psi_{2}
satisfy the estimates (
7.14
).

For the second term in (
7.15
), we have

\begin{split}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})\partial_{\alpha}u\partial_{\beta}h=&\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}h\delta^{(1)}_{x_{2}-\phi}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}\partial_{2}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}h\delta^{(0)}_{x_{2}-\phi}.\end{split}

Then we can take

\psi_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}\partial_{2}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}h,\quad\psi_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\nu_{\alpha}(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}+\mathbf{g}^{\beta\alpha}_{\lambda})(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial_{\beta}h.

Thanks to (
4.9
), Proposition
5.1
, and Corollary
5.6
, we conclude that this settings of
\psi_{0}
and
\psi_{1}
satisfy the estimate (
7.14
).

For the third term in (
7.15
), we take

\psi_{0}=\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}_{\lambda}(t,x^{\prime},\phi)\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}h.

By using (
4.9
), Proposition
5.1
, and Corollary
5.6
again, this settings of
\psi_{0}
satisfies the estimates (
7.14
).
∎

As a immediate consequence, we can obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 7.3
.

Let
f
be a normalized wave packet. Then the following estimates hold:

\|dP_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim 1,\quad\|\square_{\mathbf{g}}P_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

From Proposition
7.2
and Corollary
7.3
, a single normalized wave packet is not enough for us to construct approximate solutions to a linear wave equation. Therefore, we discuss the superposition of wave packets as follows.

7.2.2.
Superpositions of wave packets

The index
\omega
stands for the initial orientation of the wave packet at
t=-2
, which varies over a maximal collection of approximately
\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}
unit vectors separated by at least
\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. For each
\omega
, we have the orthonormal coordinate system
(x_{\omega},x^{\prime}_{\omega})
of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, where
x_{\omega}=x\cdot\omega
, and
x^{\prime}_{\omega}
are projective along
\omega
.

Next, we decompose
\mathbb{R}^{2}
into a parallel tiling of rectangles of size
(8\lambda)^{-1}
in the
x_{\omega}
direction, and
(4\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in the other directions
x^{\prime}_{\omega}
. The index
j\in\mathbb{N}
corresponds to a counting of these rectangles in this decomposition. We let
R_{\omega,j}
denote the collection of the doubles of these rectangles, and let
\Sigma_{\omega,j}
denote the element of the
\Sigma_{\omega}
foliation upon which
R_{\omega,j}
is centered.
The distinct elements of the foliation, denoted by
\Sigma_{\omega,j}
are thus separated by at least
(8\lambda)^{-1}
at
t=−2
, and thus by
(9\lambda)^{-1}
at all values of
t
, as shown in (
7.18
) below. Let
\gamma_{\omega,j}
denote the null geodesic contained in
\Sigma_{\omega,j}
which passes through the center of
R_{\omega,j}
at time
t=−2
.

We denote the slab
T_{\omega,j}
as

T_{\omega,j}=\Sigma_{\omega,j}\cap\{|x^{\prime}_{\omega}-\gamma_{\omega,j}|\leq(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\},

(7.17)

and let

\Sigma_{\omega}=\cup_{r\in\mathbb{R}}\Sigma_{\omega,r}.

For each
\omega
the slabs
T_{\omega,j}
satisfy a finite-overlap condition; indeed, slabs associated to different elements of
\Sigma_{\omega}
are disjoint, and those associated to the
same
\Sigma_{\omega}
have finite overlap in the
x^{\prime}_{\omega}
variable, since the flow restricted to any

\Sigma_{\omega,r}
is
C^{1}
close to translation. We next introduce some geometry properties for these slabs.

By (
5.2
) and (
5.29
), then the estimate

|dr_{\theta}-(\theta\cdot dx-dt)|\lesssim\epsilon_{1},

holds pointwise uniformly on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. This also implies that

|\phi_{\theta,r}(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})-\phi_{\theta,r^{\prime}}(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})-(r-r^{\prime})|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}|r-r^{\prime}|.

(7.18)

On the other hand, (
4.10
) tells us

\|d^{2}_{x^{\prime}_{\omega}}\phi_{\omega,r}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega})-d^{2}_{x^{\prime}_{\omega}}\phi_{\omega,r^{\prime}}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x^{\prime}_{\omega}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\bar{\rho}(t),

(7.19)

where set

\bar{\rho}(t)=\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x}}.

By using (
7.18
) and (
7.19
), we get

\|d_{x^{\prime}_{\omega}}\phi_{\omega,r}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega})-d_{x^{\prime}_{\omega}}\phi_{\omega,r^{\prime}}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x^{\prime}_{\omega}}}\lesssim(\epsilon_{2}+\bar{\rho}(t))^{\frac{1}{2}}|r-r^{\prime}|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For
dx_{\omega}-d\phi_{\omega,r}
is null and also
|d\mathbf{g}|\leq\bar{\rho}(t)
, this also implies Hölder-
\frac{1}{2}
bounds on
d\phi_{\omega,r}
. Therefore, we suppose that
(t,x)\in\Sigma_{\omega,r}
and
(t,y)\in\Sigma_{\omega,r^{\prime}}
, that
|x^{\prime}_{\omega}-y^{\prime}_{\omega}|\leq 2(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, and that
|r-r^{\prime}|\leq 2\lambda^{-1}
. Using (
5.2
), we can obtain

|l_{\omega}(t,x)-l_{\omega}(t,y)|\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\rho}(t)\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Due to
\dot{\gamma}_{\omega}=l_{\omega}
and
\|\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}
, any geodesic in
\Sigma_{\omega}
which intersects a slab
T_{\omega,j}
should be contained in the similar slab of half the scale.

We now introduce a lemma for superpositions of wave packets from a certain fixed time.

Lemma 7.4
.

[
[
49
]
,Lemma 8.5 and Lemma 8.6]
Let
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
,
\delta_{0}\in(0,s_{0}-\frac{7}{4})
and
0<\mu<\delta_{0}
. Let a scalar function
\bar{v}(t,x)
be formulated by

\bar{v}(t,x)=\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{4}}P_{\lambda}\sum_{\omega,j}T_{\lambda}(\psi^{\omega,j}\delta_{x_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,j}(t,x^{\prime}_{\omega})}).

Set
a=(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Then we have

\|\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{\omega,j}\|\psi^{\omega,j}\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}_{a}},\qquad\text{if}\ \ \ \ \bar{\rho}(t)\leq\epsilon_{0},

(7.20)

and

\|\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\bar{\rho}(t)\sum_{\omega,j}\|\psi^{\omega,j}\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}_{a}},\quad\text{if}\ \ \bar{\rho}(t)\geq\epsilon_{0}.

(7.21)

Remark 7.3
.

Thanks to (
7.20
) and (
7.21
), we can carry out

\|\bar{v}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\left(1+\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\bar{\rho}(t)\right)\sum_{\omega,j}\|\psi^{\omega,j}\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}_{a}}.

(7.22)

Proposition 7.5

(
[
49
]
,Proposition 8.4)
.

Let
f=\sum_{\omega,j}a_{\omega,j}f^{\omega,j}
, where
f^{\omega,j}
are normalized wave packets supported in
T_{\omega,j}
. Then we have

\|dP_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}},

(7.23)

and

\|\square_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}P_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(7.24)

Proof.

We first prove a weaker estimate comparing with (
7.23
)

\|dP_{\lambda}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(7.25)

By using (
7.22
) and replacing
P_{\lambda}
by
\lambda^{-1}\nabla P_{\lambda}
, and
\psi^{\omega,j}=a_{\omega,j}\zeta^{\omega,j}
, we have

\|\nabla P_{\lambda}f(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim(1+\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\bar{\rho}(t))\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j}.

Due to the fact
\|\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{4}_{t}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}
, we can see that

\|\nabla P_{\lambda}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j}.

(7.26)

We also need to get the similar estimate for the time derivatives. We can calculate

\partial_{t}h=\dot{\gamma}(t)(\epsilon_{0}\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{h},\quad\partial_{t}\delta(x_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,j})=\partial_{t}\phi_{\omega,j}\delta^{(1)}(x_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,j}).

For
\dot{\gamma}\in L^{\infty}_{t}
and
\partial_{t}\phi_{\omega,j}\in L^{\infty}_{t}
, then we have

\|\partial_{t}P_{\lambda}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j}.

(7.27)

Together with (
7.26
) and (
7.27
), we have proved (
7.25
).

To prove (
7.24
), we use the formula (
7.13
). Considering the right hand of (
7.13
), by using (
7.25
), we can bound the first term by

\|L(d\mathbf{g},d\tilde{P}_{\lambda}\tilde{f})\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\tilde{P}_{\lambda}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(7.28)

It only remains for us to estimate the second right term on (
7.13
). If we set

\vartheta=\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{5}{4}}P_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}\left(\sum_{\omega,j}a_{\omega,j}\cdot\sum_{m=0,1,2}\psi^{\omega,j}_{m}\delta^{(m)}_{x_{\omega}-\phi_{\omega,j}}\right),

(7.29)

we have

\|\vartheta\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}=(1+\bar{\rho}(t)\epsilon_{0}^{-1})\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j}\sum_{m=0,1,2}\lambda^{m-1}\|\psi^{\omega,j}_{m}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1+\mu}_{a}}.

By (
7.14
), we therefore get

\begin{split}&\|\vartheta\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\left(\int^{2}_{-2}[1+\bar{\rho}(t)\epsilon_{0}^{-1}]dt\right)\left(\int^{2}_{-2}\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j}\sum_{m=0,1,2}\lambda^{2(m-1)}\|\psi^{\omega,j}_{m}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1+\mu}_{a}}dt\right)\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}(\sum_{\omega,j}a^{2}_{\omega,j})^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(7.30)

Due to (
7.13
), using (
7.28
), (
7.29
), and (
7.30
), we have proved (
7.24
). Using (
7.25
) and (
7.24
), and classical energy estimates for linear wave equation, we obtain (
7.23
).
∎

7.2.3.
Matching the initial data

Although we have constructed the approximate solutions using superpositions of normalized wave packets, we also need to complete this construction, i.e. matching the initial data for the solutions.

Proposition 7.6

(
[
49
]
, Proposition 8.7)
.

Given any initial data
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{1}\times L^{2}
, there exists a function of the form

f=\sum_{\omega,j}a_{\omega,j}f^{\omega,j},

where the function
f^{\omega,j}
are normalized wave packets, such that

P_{\lambda}f(-2)=P_{\lambda}f_{0},\quad\partial_{t}P_{\lambda}f(-2)=P_{\lambda}f_{1}.

Furthermore,

\sum_{\omega,j}a_{\omega,j}^{2}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|f_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

7.2.4.
Overlap estimates

Since the foliations
\Sigma_{\omega,r}
varing with
\omega
and
r
, so a fixed
\Sigma_{\omega,r}
may intersect with other
\Sigma_{\omega^{\prime},r^{\prime}}
. As a result, we should be clear about the number of
\lambda
-slabs which contain two given points in the space-time
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Corollary 7.7

(
[
49
]
, Proposition 9.2)
.

For all points
P_{1}=(t_{1},x_{1})
and
P_{2}=(t_{2},x_{2})
in space-time
\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
\epsilon_{0}\lambda\geq 1
, the number
N_{\lambda}(P_{1},P_{2})
of slabs of scale
\lambda
that contain both
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
satisfies the bound

\begin{split}N_{\lambda}(P_{1},P_{2})\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

After the construction of approximate solutions, we still need to prove the key estimate (
7.3
).

7.2.5.
The proof of (
7.3
)

To start the proof, let us define
\mathcal{T}=\cup_{\omega,j}T_{\omega,j}
, where
T_{\omega,j}
is set in (
7.17
). We also denote
\chi_{{J}}
be a smooth cut-off function, and
\chi_{J}=1
on a set
J
.

Proposition 7.8
.

Let
t\in[-2,2]
and

f=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{T}}a_{J}\chi_{J}f_{J},

where
\sum_{J\in\mathcal{T}}a_{J}^{2}\leq 1
and
f_{J}
are normalized wave packets in
J
. Then

\|f\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\ln\lambda)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

(7.31)

Proof.

This proof follows Smith-Tataru’s paper
[
49
]
(Proposition 10.1 on page 353).

Let us first make a partition of the time-interval
[-2,2]
. By decomposition, there exists a partition
\left\{I_{j}\right\}
of the interval
[-2,2]
into disjoint subintervals
I_{j}
such that with the size of each
I_{j}
,
|I_{j}|\approx\lambda^{-1}
, and the number of subintervals
j\approx\lambda
. We claim that, by the Mean Value Theorem, there exists a number
t_{j}
such that

\|f\|^{4}_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\sum_{j}\|f\|^{4}_{L^{4}_{I_{j}}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\sum_{j}\|f(t_{j},\cdot)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{x}},

(7.32)

where
t_{j}
is located in
I_{j}
, and
|t_{j+1}-t_{j}|\approx\lambda^{-1}
. Let us explain the (
7.32
) as follows. To be simple, we let
I_{j_{0}}=[0,\lambda^{-1}]
and
I_{j_{0}+1}=[\lambda^{-1},2\lambda^{-1}]
.By mean value theorem, on
I_{j_{0}}
, we have

\|f\|^{4}_{L^{4}_{I_{j_{0}}}L^{\infty}_{x}}=\|f(t_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lambda^{-1}.

We also have

\|f\|^{4}_{L^{4}_{I_{j_{0}+1}}L^{\infty}_{x}}=\|f(t_{j_{0}+1},\cdot)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lambda^{-1}.

If
t_{j_{0}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1}
or
t_{j_{0}+1}\geq\frac{3}{2}\lambda^{-1}
, then
|t_{j_{0}+1}-t_{j_{0}}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1}
. Otherwise,
t_{j_{0}}\in[\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1},\lambda^{-1}]
and
t_{j_{0}+1}\in[\lambda^{-1},\frac{3}{2}\lambda^{-1}]
. In this case,
we combine
I_{j_{0}},I_{j_{0}+1}
together and set
I^{*}_{j_{0}}=I_{j_{0}}\cup I_{j_{0}+1}
. On the new interval
I^{*}_{j_{0}}
, we can see that

\|f\|^{4}_{L^{4}_{I^{*}_{j_{0}}}L^{\infty}_{x}}=2\lambda^{-1}\|f(t^{*}_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{x}},

and

2\|f(t^{*}_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}=\|f(t_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|f(t_{j_{0}+1},\cdot)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}.

For
f
is a contituous function, we then get

\|f(t^{*}_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}=\left\{\frac{1}{2}(\|f(t_{j_{0}},\cdot)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|f(t_{j_{0}+1},\cdot)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}},\qquad t^{*}_{j_{0}}\in[\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1},\frac{3}{2}\lambda^{-1}].

When no confusion arise, we still set
t_{j_{0}}=t^{*}_{j_{0}}\in[\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1},\frac{3}{2}\lambda^{-1}]
. On the next time-interval
I_{j_{0}+2}=[2\lambda^{-1},3\lambda^{-1}]
, we have

\|f\|^{4}_{L^{4}_{I_{j_{0}+2}}L^{\infty}_{x}}=\|f(t_{j_{0}+1},\cdot)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lambda^{-1},\quad t_{j_{0}+1}\in[2\lambda^{-1},3\lambda^{-1}].

We thus obtain
|t_{j_{0}+1}-t_{j_{0}}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{-1}
. In this way, we can decompose
[-2,2]
.

Therefore, to prove (
7.31
), and combining with (
7.32
), we only need to show that

\sum_{j}|f(t_{j},x_{j})|^{4}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\ln\lambda,

(7.33)

where
x_{j}
is arbitrarily chosen. We then set the points
P_{j}=(t_{j},x_{j})
.

Since each points lies in at most
\approx\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}
slabs, so we may assume that
|a_{J}|\geq\epsilon_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Then we decompose the sum
f=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{T}}a_{J}\chi_{J}f_{J}
dyadically with respect to the size of
a_{J}
. We next decompose the sum over
j
via a dyadic decomposition in the numbers of slabs containing
(t_{j},x_{j})
. We may assume that we are summing over
M
points
(t_{j},x_{j})
, each of which is contained in approximately
L
slabs. Then
|f(t_{j},x_{j})|\lesssim N^{-\frac{1}{2}}L
and

\sum_{j}|f(t_{j},x_{j})|^{4}\lesssim L^{4}MN^{-2}.

(7.34)

Combining (
7.33
) with (
7.34
), so we only prove

L^{4}MN^{-1}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\ln\lambda.

(7.35)

This is a counting problem, which we will prove it by calculating in two different ways. It’s based on the number
K
of pairs
(i,j)
for which
P_{i}
and
P_{j}
are contained in a common slab, counted with multiplicity. For
J\in\mathcal{T}
, we denote by
n_{J}
the number of points
P_{j}
contained in
J
. Then

K\approx\sum_{n_{J}\geq 2}n_{J}^{2}\gtrsim N^{-1}(\sum_{n_{J}\geq 2}n_{J})^{2}.

Note that
\sum_{J\in\mathcal{T}}n_{J}\approx ML
. We consider it into two cases. If

\sum_{n_{J}\geq 2}n_{J}\leq\sum_{n_{J}=1}n_{J},

then
N\approx ML
. In this case, combining with the fact that
L\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}
, then (
7.35
) holds. Otherwise, we have

K\gtrsim N^{-1}M^{2}L^{2}.

(7.36)

In this case, by using Corollary
7.7
, we obtain

K\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq M,i\neq j}|t_{i}-t_{j}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(7.37)

The sum is maximized in the case that
t_{j}
are close as possible, i.e. if the
t_{j}
are consecutive multiples of
\lambda^{-1}
. Therefore, for
M\approx\lambda
, we can update (
7.37
) as

\begin{split}K\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq M,i\neq j}|i-j|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq M,i\neq j}|i-j|^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq M,i\neq j}1)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(M\ln M)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot(M^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
=&M^{\frac{3}{2}}\epsilon_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(7.38)

Combining (
7.38
) and (
7.36
), we get (
7.35
). Therefore, we have finished the proof of Proposition
7.8
.
∎

8.
Proof of Theorem
1.3

In this section, we will prove the existence of solutions for Theorem
1.3
(uniqueness was already established in Corollary
2.12
). First, we reduce Theorem
1.3
to the case of smooth initial data with bounded frequency support (see Proposition
8.1
below). Next, we present a small data result with smoother vorticity than that in Proposition
8.1
(see Proposition
8.2
below). Finally, based on Proposition
8.2
, we develop a semi-classical method
4
4

4

This approach is inspired by Bahouri-Chemin
[
9
]
, Tataru
[
55
]
, and Ai-Ifrim-Tataru
[
2
]
.
to prove the Strichartz estimate involving the original regular vorticity in Proposition
8.1
, thereby completing the proof of Proposition
8.1
.

8.1.
Proof of Theorem
1.3

Denote
P_{j}
being the Littlewood-Paley operator with the frequency
2^{j}(j\in\mathbb{Z})
. By frequency truncation, we set a sequence of initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0j},\rho_{0j})
satisfying

\mathbf{v}_{0j}=P_{\leq j}\mathbf{v}_{0},\quad\rho_{0j}=P_{\leq j}\rho_{0},

(8.1)

where
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\rho_{0})
is stated as (
1.27
), and
P_{\leq j}=\textstyle{\sum}_{k\leq j}P_{k}
. Following (
1.18
), we define

w_{0j}=\mathrm{e}^{-\rho_{0j}}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{v}_{0j}.

(8.2)

Due to (
1.27
), we can obtain

\begin{split}\|w_{0j}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}=&\|\mathrm{e}^{-\rho_{0j}}\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{v}_{0j}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}\\
\leq&\|\mathrm{e}^{-\rho_{0j}}P_{\leq j}(\mathrm{e}^{\rho_{0}}w_{0})\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}\\
\leq&CM_{0}\mathrm{e}^{2M_{0}}.\end{split}

(8.3)

Similarly, we have

\begin{split}\|\nabla w_{0j}\|_{L^{8}}\leq&CM_{0}.\end{split}

(8.4)

Adding (
8.1
), (
8.3
), and (
8.4
), we can see

\|\mathbf{v}_{0j}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho_{0j}\|_{H^{s}}+\|w_{0j}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\nabla w_{0j}\|_{L^{8}}\leq CM_{0}\mathrm{e}^{2M_{0}}={E}(0).

(8.5)

Using (
1.14
), (
8.1
) and (
8.2
), we get

|\mathbf{v}_{0j},\rho_{0j}|\leq C_{0},\quad c_{s}|_{t=0}\geq c_{0}>0.

(8.6)

Before we give a proof of Theorem
1.3
, let us now introduce Proposition
8.1
.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
s
be stated as in Theorem
1.3
. Let (
1.14
)-(
1.27
) hold. Let
(\mathbf{v}_{0j},\rho_{0j},w_{0j})
be stated in (
8.1
) and (
8.2
). For each
j\geq 1
, consider Cauchy problem (
1.22
) with the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0j},\rho_{0j},w_{0j})
. Then for all
j\geq 1
, there exists two positive constants
T^{*}>0
and
{M}_{2}>0
(
T^{*}
and
{M}_{2}
only depends on
{M}_{0},s,C_{0}
and
c_{0}
) such that (
1.22
) has a unique solution
(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j})\in C([0,T^{*}];H_{x}^{s})
,
w_{j}\in C([0,T^{*}];H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}})
. To be precise,

(1)

the solution
\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j}
and
w_{j}
satisfy the energy estimates

\|\mathbf{v}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\rho_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|w_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\nabla w_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}L_{x}^{8}}\leq{M}_{2},

(8.7)

and

\|\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}}\leq 2+C_{0}.

(8.8)

(2)

the solution
\mathbf{v}_{j}
and
\rho_{j}
satisfy the Strichartz estimate

\|d\mathbf{v}_{j},d\rho_{j}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T^{*}]}L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq{M}_{2}.

(8.9)

(3)

for
s-\frac{3}{4}\leq r\leq\frac{11}{4}
, consider the following linear wave equation

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}_{j}}f_{j}=0,\qquad[0,T^{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
(f_{j},\partial_{t}f_{j})|_{t=0}=(f_{0j},f_{1j}),\end{cases}

(8.10)

where
(f_{0j},f_{1j})=(P_{\leq j}f_{0},P_{\leq j}f_{1})
and
(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}\times H^{r-1}_{x}
. Then there is a unique solution
f_{j}
on
[0,T^{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Moreover, for
a\leq r-(s-1)
, the following estimates

\begin{split}&\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}{f}_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T^{*}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq{M}_{3}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r-1}}),\\
&\|{f}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}{f}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq{M}_{3}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}),\end{split}

(8.11)

hold, where
{M}_{3}
is a universal constant depends on
C_{0},c_{0},M_{0},s
. Above, the similar estimate holds if we replace
\left<\nabla\right>^{a}
by
\left<\nabla\right>^{a-1}d
.

Based on Proposition
8.1
, we are ready to prove Theorem
1.3
.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
by using Proposition
8.1
.

If we set
\mathbf{U}_{j}=(p(\rho_{j}),\mathbf{v}_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}
, by Lemma
2.1
and
1.1
, for
j\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
we have

\begin{split}A^{0}(\mathbf{U}_{j})\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{j}+A^{i}(\mathbf{U}_{j})\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}_{j}=0,\\
\partial_{t}w_{j}+(\mathbf{v}_{j}\cdot\nabla)w_{j}=0.\end{split}

Then for any
j,l\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, we obtain

\begin{split}A^{0}(\mathbf{U}_{j})\partial_{t}(\mathbf{U}_{j}-\mathbf{U}_{l})+A^{i}(\mathbf{U}_{j})\partial_{i}(\mathbf{U}_{j}-\mathbf{U}_{l})=&-\{A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U}_{j})-A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U}_{l})\}\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U}_{l},\\
\partial_{t}(w_{j}-w_{l})+(\mathbf{v}_{j}\cdot\nabla)(w_{j}-w_{l})=&-\{(\mathbf{v}_{j}-\mathbf{v}_{l})\cdot\nabla\}w_{l}.\end{split}

(8.12)

Due to (
8.12
), using (
8.7
)-(
8.9
), we can show that

\|\mathbf{U}_{j}-\mathbf{U}_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H^{1}_{x}}+\|w_{j}-w_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\leq C_{{}_{2}}(\|\mathbf{v}_{0j}-\mathbf{v}_{0l}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho_{0j}-\rho_{0l}\|_{H^{s}}+\|w_{0j}-w_{0l}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}).

Here
C_{2}
is a positive constant depending on
{M}_{2}
. By Lemma
2.5
, so we get

\|\mathbf{v}_{j}-\mathbf{v}_{l},\rho_{j}-\rho_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H^{1}_{x}}+\|w_{j}-w_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\leq C_{2}(\|\mathbf{v}_{0j}-\mathbf{v}_{0l}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\rho_{0j}-\rho_{0l}\|_{H^{s}}+\|w_{0j}-w_{0l}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}).

Thus,
\{(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},w_{j})\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
H^{1}_{x}\times H^{1}_{x}\times H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}
. Let the limit be
(\mathbf{v},\rho,w)
. Then

\begin{split}(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},w_{j})\rightarrow&(\mathbf{v},\rho,w)\quad\quad\ \ \quad\text{in}\ \ H_{x}^{1}\times H_{x}^{1}\times H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(8.13)

By using (
8.7
), a subsequence of
\{(\mathbf{v}_{j},\rho_{j},w_{j})\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
is weakly convergent. Therefore, if
m\rightarrow\infty
, then

\begin{split}(\mathbf{v}_{j_{m}},\rho_{j_{m}},w_{j_{m}},\nabla w_{j_{m}})\rightharpoonup&(\mathbf{v},\rho,w,\nabla w)\quad\qquad\ \ \text{in}\ \ H_{x}^{s}\times H_{x}^{s}\times H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}\times L^{8}_{x}.\end{split}

(8.14)

Due to (
8.13
) and (
8.14
), then
(\mathbf{v},\rho,w,\nabla w)
is a strong solution of (
1.22
) in
H_{x}^{s}\times H_{x}^{s}\times H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}\times L^{8}_{x}
